## Context

- A startup snapshot (A1, M4), the code cache and AOT units must reach heap objects with no redesign. A snapshot maps, deserializes and rewrites pointers; units then reach its objects only by index or through link cells (03 §6).
- Depends on: H2 (bytecode ABI), H8 (ShapeId rule), H12 (no address identity), H22 (realms), H25 (off-heap pointers), H31 (fuses).
- Depended on by: H35 (link table, RootIndex relocations), H34 (RootIndex kind), H30 (RootRef, IntrinsicRef), H16 and H37 (facts name builtins and intrinsics by index).

## Decision

Copied from the plan row: an external-reference table. Intrinsics in fixed order. Stable indices for isolate roots, realm intrinsics, builtins, atoms, fuses and snapshot root shapes. A deterministic snapshot layout. No raw pointers in bytecode (02 §6).

```text
 build:  snapshot blob { roots[n], realm intrinsics[j], atoms[k], fuses[f], root shapes[s] }
 start:  map -> deserialize -> rewrite pointers -> fill the isolate root table -> load units
 unit:   text --RootIndex(n)--> isolate root table --> object
         text --LinkSlot(i)---> RW link cell (resolved at load or first run) --> object
```

**Index lists at M0 (as built, 55c15d0; counts and the native table re-checked at 383a317, M0 month 2 P1, and the counts again at cbae45d, M0 month 3 P2).**

## Rules

| Rule | Check | From |
|---|---|---|
| Each index list is append-only: no id is reordered, removed or reused [2026-11-04, M6-P3 B3T2 (M6-PLAN-D21, MC-w22-17):] Except `Root`: its ids are root slots, which are stable within one engine build only until A2 start (H13-Q1 (2)), and append-only from A2 start (the Freeze-table part "root slots"; the `Root` golden of H13-Q2). The M6-P0 insert of `Root::RealmFuses` before `Root::RealmKey` is that move. | the order checks in the table above (golden files; `EP9_BLESS=1` only after a review) | M0 wk4 |
| Intrinsics are created in a fixed order: two isolates give the same intrinsic objects and property order | `tests::builtins::two_isolates_have_the_same_intrinsic_property_order` | M0 wk4 |
| Bytecode holds no raw pointer: operands and constants are indices or owned literals, and the VM turns them into Values per isolate at load | `roundtrip::random_units_round_trip`; `determinism::builds_on_other_threads_agree` (same bytes from other threads) | M0 wk3 |
| No heap address reaches a hash or an order, so a snapshot layout can be deterministic | `tests::strings::ep9_hash_seed_gives_the_same_hashes_in_two_runs` (`EP9_HASH_SEED`); H12 rules | M0 wk4 |
| Each native word in a heap cell has an index form that a snapshot can write. FUNCTION +24: a bytecode info = (unit, function id); a table builtin's host info = its BuiltinId (the info's place in its module's dense `infos`, `builtins::prepare`; the info does not store the id); an embedder host function (`realm::new_host_function`: unit null, function id u32::MAX) has none yet, H13-Q5. FEEDBACK info = (unit, function id) (only bytecode infos get a vector). CONTEXT scope word (OBJECT_LAYOUT 2) = (unit, scope id) | review of each `native_of` store into a cell (H25 inventory); `tests::objects::function_cell_infos_have_an_index_form` | M2-P0 |
| Units reach snapshot objects only by root or intrinsic index, or through a link cell | H35 zero-text-relocation check; SP-Unit link-cell test | SP-Unit (M3-P1), A2 |
| Root ShapeIds are stable per engine build only; every other ShapeId is run-local (H8) | A1 snapshot test: two builds of the snapshot give the same bytes | M4 (A1) |
| One external-reference table serves the snapshot and every unit link table (03 §5) | A1 snapshot test; SP-Unit loader test | M4 (A1) |

## Consequences

- **Gains.** The snapshot, the code cache and AOT units need no object-model change. Parallel agents append ids without shifting each other's (W4-BUILTIN-IDS). Facts, hints and profiles name builtins and intrinsics by a stable index (W4-FACTS-REFS).
- **Costs.** A removed builtin or intrinsic keeps its id forever (a dead slot). Every new list needs its own golden test.
- **Follow-ups.** M1: the isolate root list, the fuse list (H31) and the external-reference table with order checks. M4: the snapshot writer and reader, and the root-shape list.

## Evidence

| Tag | Claim used here | Link |
|---|---|---|
| [R09] | Day-1 constraints: every heap-to-native pointer goes through an external-reference table; intrinsics are created deterministically; bytecode holds constant-pool indices, never raw pointers. Pointer rewriting at deserialization works on the non-moving heap | [../research/09-critic.md](../research/09-critic.md) |
| [R14] | The dual-mode design needs a startup snapshot with stable indices: roots, builtins, ShapeIds, atoms, class root shapes | [../research/14-dual-mode-architecture.md](../research/14-dual-mode-architecture.md) |
| [R17] | Deterministic ShapeIds are needed only for snapshot-created root shapes; other AOT shape guards use RW cells filled at first execution | [../research/17-aot-refutation.md](../research/17-aot-refutation.md) |

## Validation

- M0: the Rules checks above run in `cargo test` on 4 targets.
- M1 exit: every list in the table has an order check. M4 (A1): snapshot start ≥ 2x faster than without (STARTUP-SET); 100% of invalidation tests pass (07 §3).

## Open questions

| ID | Question | Needed by | Proposed default |
|---|---|---|---|
| H13-Q1 | Which lists must stay stable across engine builds, and which only within one build? A snapshot and a unit with another build id are rejected anyway (H35 S3) | M1 (index lists) | Within one build is enough for the snapshot and units. The ids that facts, hints and profiles store (builtins, intrinsics, well-known symbols, atoms) stay append-only across builds. **Filled 2026-11-04 (M6-P3 B3T2; FREEZE-M1 B3; M6-PLAN-D21, MODES-CHECK-w22 MC-w22-17): as proposed, with the root slots named.** (1) Append-only across builds: the ids that facts, hints, profiles or unit code store: `BuiltinId`, `IntrinsicId`, the well-known symbols, the atom ids (H19 "atom ids (append-only)") and the `RuntimeTable` entries (H13-Q3). The Freeze-table part "index ids (append-only)" holds them. (2) Stable within one engine build only: the root slots, that is the place of a `Root`, an atom or a single-character atom in the isolate root table. The table holds the realm roots, then the `atoms!` list, then the 256 single-character atoms (`crates/ep9_vm/src/runtime.rs:122-126`), so a `Root` append moves every atom slot, an atom append moves the 256 single-character slots, and no version covers the move. Nothing bakes a root slot today (no unit text, no snapshot; facts name builtins and intrinsics by their ids), and a snapshot or unit of another build is rejected (H35 S3). From A2 start, when unit code first loads a RootIndex (H34), root slots are append-only: an existing slot never moves. C-18 picks the form at A2 (the Freeze-table part "root slots"). (3) Root ShapeIds stay stable per engine build only (Rules) |
| H13-Q2 | `Root` has no order check: a reorder passes every test | M1 | A golden of the `Root` names, like `vm-builtin-ids.txt`. **Re-dated 2026-11-04 (M6-P3 B3T2; FREEZE-M1 B3): needed by A2 start, with the root slots (H13-Q1 (2)).** Within one build a reorder of `Root` breaks nothing that is stored: before A2 no unit, snapshot or facts file names a root slot. The golden of the `Root` names comes with C-18 at A2, when root slots become append-only. Today two tests pin the count and the last realm roots: `runtime::tests::atoms_and_roots_are_distinct` (`ROOT_COUNT` 35) and `tests::layout6::the_fuse_layout_and_the_realm_fuses_root` (`Root::RealmFuses` right before `Root::RealmKey`) |
| H13-Q3 | Is the native runtime-function table (JIT and AOT slow paths) an H13 list? | M3-P1 SP-UNIT (moved from M0 month 2 by M2-D21-FAIL) | **Filled 2026-10-23 (M3-P1 SP-UNIT): yes, an append-only list with a golden.** Unit code calls it by offset (8 bytes per entry). The list, in order: 0 `poll`, 1 `call_any`, 2 `add`, 3 `inc`, 4 `compare`, 5 `stack_overflow`, 6 `throw`, 7 `unwind` (`RuntimeTable::ENTRIES`, spike-native). `tests::native::the_runtime_table_matches_the_golden` compares names, offsets and types with `crates/ep9_vm/tests/golden/runtime-table.txt`; `a_reordered_runtime_table_fails_the_golden` is its negative control. A new entry goes at the end; `EP9_BLESS=1` only after a review of the appended lines. [Note 2026-10-27, M4-P4 integration fixer A (P3-ORCH-20 (2), SPIKEC-4 need 2): "the list, in order" above names entries 0-7, as filled; entries 8 `get_keyed` (offset 64) and 9 `set_keyed` (72) were appended in that order by M4-P3 KEYED (8140b68; the lists-table row above), and since M4-P4 SPIKEC-4 (469d1b6) Spike C calls them as the generic path of keyed sites.] [Note 2026-10-28, M5-P0 P0-VM5 (plan v10 §2.4 step 8, V10-D39, SC WI-SC0): entry 10 `deopt` (offset 80, type `RtFn2`) is appended; its M5-P0 body throws "deopt not built" and calls no recording function (MC-w15-6), and M5-P1 SPIKEC-5 builds it. The `RuntimeTable` type and the `RT_*` offsets moved out of the `spike-native` gate into `crates/ep9_vm/src/layout.rs` (C-18, V10-D3; `ep9_vm::layout::RT_DEOPT`), and VM_LAYOUT_VERSION 1 owns the offsets and the length; the `RUNTIME_TABLE` static stays gated in `native.rs`. The golden gets one appended line; the order of entries 0-9 does not change.] [Note 2026-11-02, M6-P0 VM6 (plan v11 §2.4 step 2; M6-PLAN-D43, SC D4): entry 11 `load_global` (offset 88, type `RtFn0`) is appended. It loads the global binding that the `LdaGlobal` or `LdaGlobalInsideTypeof` at the bytecode offset names (the name constant comes from the bytecode, so native code bakes no heap constant): T0's GetBindingValue of the global environment, with no IC update (no recording function, MC-w15-6). Spike C kernel 2 loads `Math` through it (M6-P1 SPIKEC-9). The golden gets one appended line (`11 88 load_global RtFn0`); entries 0-10 keep their order and offsets. The table length is not a VM_LAYOUT fact (doc 13 C-18: the unit header field `runtime_table_len`), so the append moves no version: VM_LAYOUT_VERSION 1 owns the offsets only, and the `versions.rs` and `layout.rs` doc lines say so since this step (the 2026-10-28 note above said "the offsets and the length"). Test: `tests::layout6::load_global` (spike-native).] |
| H13-Q4 | Does the global declarative record stay a plain object (`Root::Lexical`), or become a script context? | M1 (H22 realms) | Decide with H22 realms; the snapshot stores whichever exists. **Filled 2026-11-04 (M6-P3 B3T2; FREEZE-M1 B3): a plain object, as built.** H22-Q3 (filled 2026-10-23) made the global declarative record a field of the REALM record, a null-prototype object (`realmf::GLOBAL_LEXICAL`, REALM +16, `crates/ep9_vm/src/object.rs:254-256`), mirrored by the root `Root::Lexical`. Global-site ICs and the facts export read its shape as they read any object's (`crates/ep9_vm/src/ic.rs:1348`), so a snapshot (A1) stores it as an ordinary object. A script context would change the REALM field and these readers: a rewrite of this fill and of H22-Q3 |
| H13-Q5 | How does a snapshot write FUNCTION +24 for a host function? An embedder host function's info is a boxed FunctionInfo in `rt.infos` (unit null, function id u32::MAX, the same pair for all of them), and its `HostFn` has no index before the external-reference table exists. A table builtin's info does not store its BuiltinId | M4 (A1 snapshot writer) | Open. Proposed: an external-reference index for the embedder's `HostFn` (the one table of 03 §5), and a BuiltinId field (or module and index) in a table builtin's FunctionInfo, set by `builtins::prepare` |

## Freeze table

README §3.4 form (the Freeze point field above; plan v11 §5.7, M6-PLAN-D26). Part names hold no `,` and no ` and `, because the Frozen check reads a Status line that lists several parts (`tools/xtask/src/exit/freeze.rs`).

| Part | What it holds | Freeze point | After the freeze point | Check |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| rules | The Decision, the Rules rows, the index-list table (which lists are H13 lists, where each lives and its order check) and the fills H13-Q1 (what is stable across builds), H13-Q3 (the `RuntimeTable` is an H13 list) and H13-Q4 | M1 exit | Frozen-part rules (README §3.2) | the order checks of the index-list table; the Rules rows |
| index ids (append-only) | The ids of the lists that H13-Q1 (1) names: `BuiltinId`, `IntrinsicId`, the well-known symbols, the `RuntimeTable` entries (index, offset 8 x index, type; doc 13 §5.4 Kind A) [2026-11-05, M6-P4 fixer (B-CHECK F11): also the bytecode `RuntimeId` list, which H13-Q1 (1) does not name: H02-Q5 makes it an H13 index list and append-only; an append also bumps `ABI_VERSION`, which H02 holds] and the atom ids (H19 "atom ids (append-only)" holds them). A new id goes at the end; an existing id never moves, goes away or is reused. The `RuntimeTable` append rule as built (plan v11 inputs item 24; M6-PLAN-D43, M6-PLAN-D44): its golden gets one line at the end (`crates/ep9_vm/tests/golden/runtime-table.txt`); the H20 golden prints the table one line per field (the `RuntimeTable field nn` lines of `crates/ep9_vm/tests/golden/h20-core.txt`), so an append adds lines there and changes none; the table length is not a VM_LAYOUT fact (the `VM_LAYOUT_VERSION` doc, `crates/ep9_vm/src/versions.rs:109-118`; doc 13 C-18), so an append moves no version | None | an append is a fill and G8 never counts it; a change of an existing id is a rewrite | the goldens and swap tests of the index-list table |
| root slots | The place of each `Root`, atom and single-character atom in the isolate root table (H13-Q1 (2)): stable within one engine build until A2; from A2 start append-only (an existing slot never moves), in the form that C-18 picks at A2, with the `Root` golden of H13-Q2 [2026-11-06, M7-P0 AOT0 (plan v12 V12-D13; MODES-CHECK MC-w22-17.3, MC-w27-6; doc 13 C-18); fill, before the freeze point:] **The C-18 form.** "A2 start" is the merge commit of M7-P1 A2-START into `m2/m7` (V12-D13); the orchestrator writes the Frozen line of this part right after that merge. (1) A root slot is an index into the isolate root table: unit code loads slot n at byte 8n of the table (H34 `RootIndex(n)`), so the slot number is the contract, not a `Root` enum value. (2) The slots at A2 start are the layout of that commit: the realm roots (`Root` values below `REALM_ROOT_COUNT`), then `Root::RealmKey`, then the `atoms!` list, then the 256 single-character atoms (`crates/ep9_vm/src/runtime.rs:122-126`). The `Root` golden of H13-Q2 records each slot (its number and the root, atom or character in it). (3) From A2 start a slot never moves, is never reused and never holds another root. A root or an atom added after A2 start takes the next free slot at the end of the table, after the last slot of the golden, never a slot inside a list; so after the first such append a list is no longer one slot range, and code that walks a list by slot range (`Runtime::clear_realm_roots` clears the realm roots `roots[..REALM_ROOT_COUNT]` at a new realm's init) walks each of its ranges [M7-P0 fixer r2, JIT reviewer F3: was `realm::switch`, which loads the intrinsics by `IntrinsicId`]. [M7-P0 fixer r2, JIT reviewer F3:] The root table code of today does not have this form: a `Root` is `roots[r]`, an atom `roots[ATOM_ROOTS + id]`, and the 256 character atoms start at `ATOM_ROOTS + Atom::ALL.len()` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/runtime.rs:122-126`, `crates/ep9_vm/src/runtime.rs:466-497`), so one new `Root` moves every atom slot and one new atom moves the 256 character slots. So a root or an atom append after A2 start waits for the code of a second slot range (an orchestrator route: plan v12 §4.1 gives A2-START only the `Root` golden test) [2026-11-06, M7-P0 fixer r3b (MODES-CHECK MC-w30-7; M6-ORCH-145 item 4); clarify: the route text above is out of date. Plan v12 §4.1 task 3 and the A2-START grant now give A2-START the second slot range for roots and atoms appended after A2 start (the root and atom slot mapping of `crates/ep9_vm/src/runtime.rs`: `Runtime::root`, `Runtime::atom`, the base of the 256 character slots and `clear_realm_roots`), with the checks of item (5). So A2-START lands this code with the golden; no other route holds it] [2026-11-07, M7-P1 (JIT reviewer F5); clarify: since A2 start (cd4e4766) the code has this form: `SLOT_MAP` and `APPENDED` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/runtime.rs`) give each root and atom appended after A2 start a slot after the 256 character slots, so an append moves no slot; the sentence above on "the root table code of today" is the state before A2 start]. A retired root keeps its slot, and the slot then holds undefined. (4) The slot of each root, and every other fact that unit code bakes, is the same under every `ep9_vm` Cargo feature set of one commit: `Root`, `atoms!` and the root table have no `cfg`, because the build id names no feature (the C-19 rule in the H35 "Build id" row; MC-w27-6). (5) The checks that A2-START lands with the golden: the `Root` golden test; the same golden in the jit and the no-jit builds; an append test (a scratch root appended at the end adds one golden line and changes none). (6) A unit of another engine build goes to SOURCE (H35 S4 step 3), so a slot is read only by the build that wrote the unit; a later relax of that step (the H35 "Reopens if" item) adds a root-slot count to the unit header first, read as `runtime_table_len` is | A2 start | append-only: an append is a fill | the count tests of H13-Q2 today; the `Root` golden from A2 |
| snapshot build | The A1 snapshot writer and reader, the snapshot root-shape list, the external-reference table (03 §5) and H13-Q5 | M4 exit | Frozen-part rules (README §3.2) | the A1 snapshot gates (M4) |

## Reopens if

- The A1 snapshot gate fails (M4), or SP-Unit cannot reach an object by index.
- S3 (moving GC) starts: pointer rewriting and RW cells must then follow moves (H12).

They are the first recording entries: native code passes the site in (the FunctionInfo of its own frame `fp`, and the feedback slot as an operand), and the entry checks that the instruction at `off` is of its kind and names that slot, else it throws an internal error; it never finds a site by itself. `alloc_site` runs the object-model part of T0's array-literal cold path (`literal_site::array_site`: the ALLOC_SITE handler, its birth kind and count, ARRAY +32); `clone_widen` runs the field widening of T0's clone cold path (`field_rep::widen_all` of the result shape) and records no case (`crates/ep9_vm/src/literal_site.rs` `native_alloc_site`, `native_clone_widen`; `crates/ep9_vm/src/native.rs` `rt_alloc_site`, `rt_clone_widen`). `crates/ep9_vm/tests/golden/runtime-table.txt` gains exactly 2 lines at its end (`12 96 alloc_site RtFn2`, `13 104 clone_widen RtFn3`); entries 0-11 keep their order and offsets.
